**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01G1P9GA47Sp9CYqqojLvztG`

```
The theme system is centralized in `src/theme.rs` and follows a two-stage loading process: raw YAML data is parsed into a `ThemeData` struct, then resolved into a `Theme` struct where hex strings are converted to `Color` objects for rendering.

### Theme Definition & Data Structures
*   **Color Struct**: [src/theme.rs#L160-L226](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L160-L226) - Defines `Color` as RGBA (u8) with hex parsing and `to_argb_u32` conversion for the renderer.
*   **Theme Struct**: [src/theme.rs#L416-L427](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L416-L427) - The final resolved theme containing sub-themes like `EditorTheme`, `SyntaxTheme`, `SidebarTheme`, etc.
*   **ThemeData (YAML Schema)**: [src/theme.rs#L229-L412](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L229-L412) - The `serde`-driven structs that match the YAML file structure.

### Theme Loading & Parsing
*   **Loading Logic**: [src/theme.rs#L80-L93](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L80-L93) - `load_theme` searches for user-defined themes in `~/.config/token-editor/themes/` before falling back to built-ins.
*   **YAML Parsing**: [src/theme.rs#L752-L768](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L752-L768) - `Theme::from_yaml` uses `serde_yaml` to parse content and then `Theme::from_data` to resolve colors.
*   **Built-in Themes**: [src/theme.rs#L15-L46](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L15-L46) - Themes like `dark.yaml` are embedded into the binary using `include_str!`.

### Applying Themes to Rendering
*   **Color Conversion**: Sub-themes provide `to_argb_u32()` methods or are manually converted during render loops to be used by the `Frame` buffer.
*   **Editor Rendering**: [src/view/mod.rs#L250-L300](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L250-L300) - Colors are pulled from `model.theme` (e.g., `theme.editor.background`) and passed to drawing primitives like `fill_rect_px`.
*   **Syntax Highlighting**: [src/theme.rs#L711-L750](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L711-L750) - `SyntaxTheme::color_for_highlight` maps Tree-sitter highlight IDs to theme colors.
*   **Drawing Primitives**: [src/view/frame.rs#L97-L263](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/frame.rs#L97-L263) - `Frame` provides the low-level `fill_rect`, `blend_pixel`, and `draw_bordered_rect` methods that consume the theme colors.
```

{
  "content": "1: //! Theme system for the editor\n2: //!\n3: //! Provides YAML-based theming support with compile-time embedded themes\n4: //! and user-defined themes from config directories.\n5: //!\n6: //! Theme loading priority:\n7: //! 1. User config: `~/.config/token-editor/themes/{id}.yaml`\n8: //! 2. Embedded: Built-in themes compiled into binary\n9: \n10: use std::path::Path;\n11: \n12: use serde::Deserialize;\n13: \n14: // Embed theme YAML files at compile time\n15: pub const DEFAULT_DARK_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/dark.yaml\");\n16: pub const FLEET_DARK_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/fleet-dark.yaml\");\n17: pub const GITHUB_DARK_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/github-dark.yaml\");\n18: pub const GITHUB_LIGHT_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/github-light.yaml\");\n19: \n20: /// A built-in theme entry\n21: pub struct BuiltinTheme {\n22:     /// Stable identifier for config (e.g. \"default-dark\", \"fleet-dark\")\n23:     pub id: &'static str,\n24:     /// Embedded YAML content\n25:     pub yaml: &'static str,\n26: }\n27: \n28: /// Registry of all built-in themes\n29: pub const BUILTIN_THEMES: &[BuiltinTheme] = &[\n30:     BuiltinTheme {\n31:         id: \"default-dark\",\n32:         yaml: DEFAULT_DARK_YAML,\n33:     },\n34:     BuiltinTheme {\n35:         id: \"fleet-dark\",\n36:         yaml: FLEET_DARK_YAML,\n37:     },\n38:     BuiltinTheme {\n39:         id: \"github-dark\",\n40:         yaml: GITHUB_DARK_YAML,\n41:     },\n42:     BuiltinTheme {\n43:         id: \"github-light\",\n44:         yaml: GITHUB_LIGHT_YAML,\n45:     },\n46: ];\n47: \n48: /// Where the theme came from\n49: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n50: pub enum ThemeSource {\n51:     /// User-defined theme in ~/.config/token-editor/themes/\n52:     User,\n53:     /// Built-in theme embedded in binary\n54:     Builtin,\n55: }\n56: \n57: /// Information about an available theme\n58: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n59: pub struct ThemeInfo {\n60:     /// Stable identifier (e.g., \"default-dark\", \"my-custom-theme\")\n61:     pub id: String,\n62:     /// Display name from YAML (e.g., \"Default Dark\")\n63:     pub name: String,\n64:     /// Where this theme is loaded from\n65:     pub source: ThemeSource,\n66: }\n67: \n68: /// Load a theme from a YAML file\n69: pub fn from_file(path: &Path) -> Result<Theme, String> {\n70:     let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)\n71:         .map_err(|e| format!(\"Failed to read theme file {}: {}\", path.display(), e))?;\n72:     Theme::from_yaml(&content)\n73: }\n74: \n75: /// Load theme by id with priority: user → builtin\n76: ///\n77: /// Searches in order:\n78: /// 1. `~/.config/token-editor/themes/{id}.yaml`\n79: /// 2. Embedded builtin themes\n80: pub fn load_theme(id: &str) -> Result<Theme, String> {\n81:     // Try user themes directory\n82:     if let Some(user_dir) = crate::config_paths::themes_dir() {\n83:         let user_path = user_dir.join(format!(\"{}.yaml\", id));\n84:         if user_path.exists() {\n85:             tracing::info!(\"Loading user theme from {}\", user_path.display());\n86:             return from_file(&user_path);\n87:         }\n88:     }\n89: \n90:     // Fall back to builtin\n91:     tracing::info!(\"Loading builtin theme: {}\", id);\n92:     Theme::from_builtin(id)\n93: }\n94: \n95: /// List all available themes from all sources\n96: ///\n97: /// Returns themes grouped by source, with duplicates resolved by priority:\n98: /// user themes override builtins with the same id.\n99: pub fn list_available_themes() -> Vec<ThemeInfo> {\n100:     let mut themes = Vec::new();\n[... omitted lines 101 to 1249 ...]",

      "Sitemap\n\n[Open in app](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.medium.reader&referrer=utm_source%3DmobileNavBar&source=post_page---top_nav_layout_nav-----------------------------------------)\n\n[](https://medium.com/?source=post_page---top_nav_layout_nav-----------------------------------------)\n\n[Write](https://medium.com/m/signin?operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2Fnew-story&source=---top_nav_layout_nav-----------------------new_post_topnav------------------)\n\n[Search](https://medium.com/search?source=post_page---top_nav_layout_nav-----------------------------------------)\n\n# **The Best Dark VS Code Themes for 2025: My Top 3 Picks**\n\nJared Christensen\n\n2 min read\n\n·\n\nDec 12, 2024\n\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fvote%2Fp%2F3b510496e9e1&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fjareddesign.medium.com%2Fthe-best-dark-vs-code-themes-for-2025-my-top-3-picks-3b510496e9e1&user=Jared+Christensen&userId=d1f761acbce4&source=---header_actions--3b510496e9e1---------------------clap_footer------------------)\n\n\\--\n\n2\n\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fbookmark%2Fp%2F3b510496e9e1&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fjareddesign.medium.com%2Fthe-best-dark-vs-code-themes-for-2025-my-top-3-picks-3b510496e9e1&source=---header_actions--3b510496e9e1---------------------bookmark_footer------------------)\n\nListen\n\nShare\n\nIf you’re like me, you spend hours in Visual Studio Code every day. A good theme isn’t just about aesthetics — it’s about reducing eye strain and keeping your workspace inspiring. Dark themes are my go-to, and after trying a bunch, these three are the ones I keep coming back to.\n\n### [**Catppuccin**](https://github.com/catppuccin/vscode)\n\nIt offers multiple “flavors” (color schemes) to suit your mood, but my favorite is **Macchiato** . It has rich, warm tones and just the right amount of contrast for extended coding sessions. The pastel accents look great without being too bright, and the overall design feels modern yet calming.\n\nPress enter or click to view image in full size\n\nCatppuccin Macchiato\n\nWhat I love about Catppuccin is how customizable it is. You can tweak it to match your setup perfectly. The team behind it has done a fantastic job balancing aesthetics with usability.\n\n### [**Minimal Kiwi**](https://github.com/pranjal-barnwal/minimal-kiwi/tree/main)\n\nIf you’re into sleek, distraction-free themes, **Minimal Kiwi** is a fantastic option. It has a minimalist design with dark backgrounds and vibrant green highlights that make your syntax pop.\n\nPress enter or click to view image in full size\n\n**Minimal Kiwi**\n\nWhat I love most about Minimal Kiwi is how it stays out of your way. The colors are understated but functional, making it perfect for those who value simplicity over flash.\n\n### [**One Dark Pro**](https://github.com/Binaryify/OneDark-Pro/tree/master)\n\n**One Dark Pro** is one of the most popular VS Code themes for a reason. Inspired by the classic Atom One Dark theme, it offers great contrast, vibrant highlights, and a clean layout that works in any coding environment.\n\nPress enter or click to view image in full size\n\n**One Dark Pro**\n\nThis was my go-to theme for years, and I still come back to it when I want something familiar and reliable. It’s polished, professional, and just works.\n\n[Vscode Extension](https://medium.com/tag/vscode-extension?source=post_page-----3b510496e9e1---------------------------------------)\n\n[Vscode](https://medium.com/tag/vscode?source=post_page-----3b510496e9e1---------------------------------------)\n\n[Color Theme](https://medium.com/tag/color-theme?source=post_page-----3b510496e9e1---------------------------------------)\n\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fvote%2Fp%2F3b510496e9e1&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fjareddesign.medium.com%2Fthe-best-dark-vs-code-themes-for-2025-my-top-3-picks-3b510496e9e1&user=Jared+Christensen&userId=d1f761acbce4&source=---footer_actions--3b510496e9e1---------------------clap_footer------------------)\n\n\\--     "title": "10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 - GitKraken",

      "Section Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025\nContent:\n[](https://www.gitkraken.com/author/gamergirlie)\n[#### Allison Wheeler](https://www.gitkraken.com/author/gamergirlie)\nJanuary 19, 2025\nProgramming is an art and a science, and while we often focus on things like performance and integrations, there’s something else that deserves attention— **aesthetics** .\nOkay, maybe not *just* as crucial. But the way our tools look and feel can impact productivity and comfort. A well-chosen [Visual Studio Code](https://code.visualstudio.com/) (VS Code) theme isn’t just about style—it enhances readability, reduces eye strain, and makes long coding sessions more enjoyable.\nWhether you’re looking for a high-contrast dark theme or a softer, eye-friendly palette, there’s a perfect fit for everyone. In this article, we’re exploring the **top VS Code color themes for 2025** to help you find the best one for your workflow.\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > What are VS Code Themes?\nContent:\nWhen you first launch VS Code, it greets you with a default theme – which is nice, but it’s pretty basic. Why settle for default when you can customize? VS Code themes can change how you interact with your code, with different colors and contrasts can help you quickly skim through code, reduce eye strain, and cater to accessibility needs.\nWhether you stick to one theme forever or love to switch things up now and then, changing your theme can rejuvenate your IDE and make coding a visually enjoyable experience.\n**Updated for 2025:** *We’ve refreshed this list with the latest and most popular VS Code themes for 2025. Explore the newest picks below!*\n ... \nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Catppuccin\nContent:\nFan-favorite Catppuccin has 4 creamy pastel color options that blend beautifully to create a soothing coding environment. This theme, available in three different dark modes and one light, is especially good for long hours of coding, providing a soft, gentle background that reduces strain without sacrificing clarity.\n[**Check out Catppuccin**](https://catppuccin.com/)\n ... \nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Dracula\nContent:\nDracula is the go-to theme for many devss for its bold use of contrast and vibrant colors that make different elements of the code stand out distinctly. But it’s not just about looks – this vampiric theme helps reduce eye strain in low-light conditions.\n[**Visit Dracula Theme**](https://draculatheme.com/visual-studio-code)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Ayu\nContent:\nAyu is a modern, minimalistic theme designed for coders who appreciate soft, well-balanced colors. Available in three variations—Dark, Mirage, and Light—it provides flexibility while maintaining a clean, distraction-free workspace for developers who love simplicity.\n[**Explore Ayu**](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=teabyii.ayu)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Dainty\nContent:\nSleek, minimal, and modern—Dainty is perfect for developers who prefer a refined look without excess visual noise. Available in both light and dark variations, it provides a clean and elegant environment for writing code.\n[**Discover Dainty**](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=KacperBiedka.one-candy-dark)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > GitHub Theme\nContent:\nGitHub Theme mirrors the colors of GitHub, providing a seamless transition from web to editor. It has various GitHub Light and Dark themes, including support for various forms of color blindness, making it one of the most accessible themes available, ensuring that everyone can code comfortably.\n[**Get the GitHub Theme**](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=GitHub.github-vscode-theme)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Atom One Dark\nContent:\nAtom One Dark, one of the best-rated One Dark theme ports on the marketplace, features full Workbench theming. It offers a fine balance between contrast and saturation, enhancing readability without overwhelming the eyes. It’s a solid choice for developers who spend long hours coding and need a theme that can keep up.\n[**View Atom One Dark**](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=akamud.vscode-theme-onedark)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Houston\nContent:\nHouston is designed with a space-inspired aesthetic, featuring cool blues, minty greens, and soft purples that reflect the vastness and mystery of the cosmos. It’s perfect for those who want their workspace to be as inspiring as the universe.\n[**Try Houston**](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=astro-build.houston)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Night Owl\nContent:\nNight Owl is crafted with a focus on reducing blue light, making it ideal for developers who work late into the night. Its carefully chosen color palette helps prevent eye strain and maintains focus.\n[**Explore Night Owl**](https://vscodethemes.com/e/sdras.night-owl/night-owl)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > 10 Trending VS Code Themes > Matcha\nContent:\nMatcha offers a unique blend of earthy green and gray tones, evoking a sense of calm and focus essential for effective coding. It’s refreshing and different, perfect for those looking to add a touch of “cottage-core” personality to their editor.\n[**Check out Matcha**](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=lucafalasco.matcha)\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > ... > How to change your color theme in VS Code?\nContent:\nTo change your [color theme in Visual Studio Code](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/getstarted/themes) , you can open the Color Theme picker by navigating through File > Preferences > Theme > Color Theme on Windows or Code > Settings > Theme > Color Theme on macOS. Alternatively, you can use the keyboard shortcut Ctrl+K Ctrl+T on Windows or Cmd+K Cmd+T on macOS to open the theme picker directly.\nOnce open, you can browse through the themes using the cursor keys and select the one you prefer by pressing Enter. The changes will apply immediately to your workspace.​\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > ... > Which VS Code theme is best for eye strain?\nContent:\nThere are many themes that implement specific colors meant to reduce eye strain, but the [High Contrast theme](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/editor/accessibility) in Visual Studio Code is a good starting point, especially under various lighting conditions or for those with certain visual impairments.\nThis theme enhances visibility and reduces glare, which can help minimize eye strain during extended periods of coding​.\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > ... > Where are VS Code themes stored?\nContent:\nVS Code color themes are [stored within your user settings](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/getstarted/themes) . Typically, when you install a new theme, it modifies the `workbench.colorTheme` setting in your `settings.json` file located in your user profile directory. This file controls the appearance settings across your VS Code environment. You can find and modify theme settings directly in this file if you wish to make more detailed customizations​.\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > ... > Can I create my own VS Code color themes?\nContent:\nYes! It’s easy to create your own custom VS Code color themes. Start by customizing your colors in your user settings, then generate a theme definition with the file in the Developer: Generate Color Theme From Current Settings command. From there, VS Code’s Yeoman [extension generator](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/get-started/your-first-extension) will help you generate the rest of the extension.\nYou can learn more by checking out the [Create a new Color Theme](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-guides/color-theme) topic in VS Code’s Extension API section.\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > Final Thoughts\nContent:\nIncorporating a new VS Code Color theme like these into your VS Code setup can dramatically improve your coding environment, making it a personalized space that not only looks good but also enhances your ability to work effectively.\nWhile you’re playing around with colors and fonts, don’t forget to enhance your coding experience with [GitLens](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=eamodio.gitlens) , which can add insightful annotations and powerful source control management tools directly in your editor! GitLens is part of the [GitKraken DevEx platform](https://www.gitkraken.com/devex) , providing powerful source control management, deeper Git insights, and seamless team collaboration—all within your editor! Whether you’re coding solo or as part of a team, these themes and tools can help you code better, faster, and with more style.\n**Like this post? Share it!\n**\nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > Final Thoughts > **Read More Articles**\nContent:\n[](https://www.gitkraken.com/blog/we-measured-ai-impact-for-12-months-heres-what-actually-happened)\n ... \nSection Title: ... > [6 Underused Git Commands That Solve Real Developer Problems](https://www.gitkraken.com/blo...\nContent:\nFebruary 9, 2026\nDiscover 6 powerful Git commands most developers don’t know about. ... \nSection Title: 10 Popular VS Code Color Themes in 2025 > ... > GitKraken Browser Extension\nContent:\nYour bridge between apps\nTrack PRs – View PRs in GitKraken or VS Code – Work More     "url": "https://codementorio.medium.com/best-text-editor-atom-vs-sublime-vs-visual-studio-code-vs-vim-85b651d36665",
    "title": "Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim | by Codementor | Medium",
    "excerpts": [
      "Section Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim\nContent:\nCodementor\n10 min read\n·\nDec 28, 2016\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fvote%2Fp%2F85b651d36665&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fcodementorio.medium.com%2Fbest-text-editor-atom-vs-sublime-vs-visual-studio-code-vs-vim-85b651d36665&user=Codementor&userId=894f169d08ac&source=---header_actions--85b651d36665---------------------clap_footer------------------)\n--\n5\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fbookmark%2Fp%2F85b651d36665&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fcodementorio.medium.com%2Fbest-text-editor-atom-vs-sublime-vs-visual-studio-code-vs-vim-85b651d36665&source=---header_actions--85b651d36665---------------------bookmark_footer------------------)\nListen\nShare\nPress enter or click to view image in full size\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim\nContent:\nWith so many programming text editors out there today, the seemingly simple task of choosing one suddenly becomes daunting and overwhelming.\nWhile a straightforward answer to the “what is the best text editor for developers?” question doesn’t exist, in this post, I will share with you a side by side comparison between four of most popular ones out there: Atom, Sublime, Visual Studio Code, and Vim.\nAfter reading the list of pros and cons, I hope you will have enough information to make a choice that best fits your needs.\n*Disclaimer: As with any comparison, some of these views are subjective and are mostly based on my personal preferences. I’m someone who writes for the web (JS, CSS, HTML, etc.) using Sublime for Mac, so my perspective is a bit skewed towards what I’m used to. That said, I’ve tried to be as objective as possible.*\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Atom\nContent:\nUrl: https://atom.io/\nCost: Free (MIT Licence)\nDeveloper: GitHub\nPlatforms: OSX, Windows, Linux\nAtom is described as:\nA hackable text editor for the 21st Century\nAtom is a relative newcomer to the world of text editors but it’s gained huge momentum since it was first released in 2014. Let’s start by reviewing its main features:\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Atom > Packages\nContent:\nPress enter or click to view image in full size\nAbility to add additional features to an edit is very important, and this is an area that Atom shines at. The package manager is installed by default and to make things even better, all packages are hosted on Github.\nAt the time of writing, they had a whopping 6,452 packages and themes available! Packages are so fundamental to Atom, that core features like Tree View and Settings View are simply pre-installed packages.\n ... \nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Sublime\nContent:\nUrl: https://www.sublimetext.com/\nCost: $70 license fee with free trial\nDeveloper: Jon Skinner former Google Engineer\nPlatforms: OSX, Windows, Linux\nSublime was released back in 2007, so it’s had almost a decade to mature. According to Stackoverflow’s 2016 developer survey it’s the [3rd most popular Development Environment](http://stackoverflow.com/research/developer-survey-2016) .\nV3 Beta is its latest version and while it’s been in beta for a long time now, don’t let that concern you. The product is actually very stable.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Sublime > Packages\nContent:\nAny Sublime user will tell you the first package they install is Sublime Package Control plugin. I think we all secretly hope that Sublime 3 will add this by default, but for now, you simply have to go to [the installation page on their website](https://packagecontrol.io/installation) and copy some incomprehensible Python and… Presto! You can now install any package easily from within Sublime.\nLike Atom, Sublime has a lot of packages and themes! For me the essentials are:\n[SideBarEnhancements](https://packagecontrol.io/packages/SideBarEnhancements) : without this, renaming/moving/duplicating files is not supported.\n[SublimeLinter](https://packagecontrol.io/packages/SublimeLinter) : To add linters for different languages\n[GitGutter](https://packagecontrol.io/packages/GitGutter) — Shows git status on the left side next to line numbers.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Sublime > Packages\nContent:\nThere’s plenty more to choose from, too. Overall, Sublime’s package support is very good, it’s just a shame that you have to jump through that one hoop at the beginning to even get started with it.\n ... \nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Sublime > Customization\nContent:\nAgain, Sublime is very much like Atom. The power to control aspects such as “save on lost focus”, “trim whitespace” and more is available. The configuration files are simple JSON and there’s a whole host of hidden settings waiting for you to play with.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Sublime > Performance\nContent:\nHere’s where Sublime outperforms Atom — opening, closing, searching, etc is very smooth and fast. The only issue I’ve ever faced with performance is from badly performing plugins — but even then you’ll get a warning message when a plugin seems to be taking too long.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Sublime > Verdict\nContent:\nAs I mentioned at the start, I’m a long time Sublime user so my opinions here are likely to be slightly biased, but in general, Sublime is a flexible and fast editor. There’s a reason it still makes the top 3 Development Environment in 2016.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Visual Studio Code\nContent:\nUrl: https://code.visualstudio.com/\nCost: Free\nDeveloper: Microsoft\nPlatforms: OSX, Windows, Linux\nVisual Studio Code (VSCode) is a relative newcomer to the world of text editors. It was only released last year (April 2015), but it has already been picking up a lot of traction. Microsoft has done a great job of creating a powerful and flexible cross-platform editor that’s piquing a lot of interest from developers.\n ... \nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Visual Studio Code > Customization\nContent:\nLike the previous two editors, the expected customization features are there — all the necessary wrapping, indenting, theming, language tweaks, etc.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Visual Studio Code > Performance\nContent:\nAs mentioned before, although VSCode (like Atom) is built on Node.js, Electron, HTML, and CSS, it definitely feels fast (unlike Atom). I didn’t experience any lags when opening/changing files. Searching was also fast. I believe one of the differences between Atom and VSCode is that the UI Editor is built on Monaco (from Visual Studio Online), which might be the explanation for the performance difference. In any case, the performance is definitely on par with Sublime.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Visual Studio Code > Verdict\nContent:\nOverall I was very impressed with VSCode, to the point that I’ve considered moving to it more permanently. I’m still yet to take that step to fully dive in, but I think it would be a nice holiday project to configure it to meet similar standards I am used to in Sublime. After that, I think I really could stick with it for longer. The Git integration and in-editor debugger are great features that I’ve struggled with in Sublime but worked almost immediately in VSCode.\n ... \nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Vim > Packages\nContent:\nAt over 14,000 packages, Vim has one for everything! Tree explorers, syntax highlighters, theming, Git integration, etc. It’s all there, in multiple versions. Vim is incredibly flexible and powerful. However as with all other editors mentioned above, knowing the best plugins to install requires some insider knowledge and recommendations.\nPersonally, I’ve found going off the most popular plugins on http://vimawesome.com/ as a starting point.\n ... \nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Vim > Customization\nContent:\nVim is amazingly customizable. If you Google search for `.vimrc` you'll find a lot of examples of preconfigured Vim configuration files. In short, anything is pretty much possible in Vim.\n ... \nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Vim > Verdict\nContent:\nVim is as raw of an editor as you can get. It can be an incredibly fast, efficient development environment if you can have the patience to learn the commands. There’s a great online game http://vim-adventures.com/ which helps to teach the basic commands, like moving around files using `h` , `j` , `k` and `l` keys.\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Final Verdict\nContent:\nAll of the above editors have their pros and cons. Personally, I’d say that in your developer’s career, you should give each one of them a shot for at least a week to see for yourself what works and what does not work for you. I hope that summarizing those four most popular text editors will make for a good starting point when considering making an editor change.\nWhat did we miss out? And what’s your favorite editor? Let us know in the comments below.\n*This article was originally published on* [*Codementor*](https://www.codementor.io/mattgoldspink/tutorials/best-text-editor-atom-sublime-vim-visual-studio-code-du10872i7) *by* [*Matt Goldspink*](https://www.codementor.io/mattgoldspink) *.*\n[Web Development](https://medium.com/tag/web-development?source=post_page-----85b651d36665---------------------------------------)\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Final Verdict\nContent:\n[Coding](https://medium.com/tag/coding?source=post_page-----85b651d36665---------------------------------------)\n[Programming](https://medium.com/tag/programming?source=post_page-----85b651d36665---------------------------------------)\n[Developer](https://medium.com/tag/developer?source=post_page-----85b651d36665---------------------------------------)\n[Tech](https://medium.com/tag/tech?source=post_page-----85b651d36665---------------------------------------)\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fvote%2Fp%2F85b651d36665&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fcodementorio.medium.com%2Fbest-text-editor-atom-vs-sublime-vs-visual-studio-code-vs-vim-85b651d36665&user=Codementor&userId=894f169d08ac&source=---footer_actions--85b651d36665---------------------clap_footer------------------)\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Atom vs Sublime vs Visual Studio Code vs Vim > Final Verdict\nContent:\n-- [](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fvote%2Fp%2F85b651d36665&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fcodementorio.medium.com%2Fbest-text-editor-atom-vs-sublime-vs-visual-studio-code-vs-vim-85b651d36665&user=Codementor&userId=894f169d08ac&source=---footer_actions--85b651d36665---------------------clap_footer------------------)\n--\n5\n[](https://medium.com/m/signin?actionUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fmedium.com%2F_%2Fbookmark%2Fp%2F85b651d36665&operation=register&redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fcodementorio.medium.com%2Fbest-text-editor-atom-vs-sublime-vs-visual-studio-code-vs-vim-85b651d36665&source=---footer_actions--85b651d36665---------------------bookmark_footer------------------)\nSection Title: Best Text Editor? Content:
[](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/main/assets/palette/macchiato.png)
[](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/stargazers) [](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/releases/latest) [](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/issues) [](https://discord.com/servers/907385605422448742)
Catppuccin is a community-driven pastel theme that aims to be the middle ground between low and high-contrast themes. It consists of 4 soothing warm flavors with 26 eye-candy colors each, perfect for coding, designing, and much more! In addition, this repository tracks the development of the actual color palette, [**the project's documentation**](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/tree/main/docs) , organization-wide assets, resources and code samples for maintainers/developers.
[](https://github.com/catppuccin)  [](https://www.npmjs.com/org/catppuccin)  [](https://discord.com/servers/907385605422448742)  [](https://twitter.com/catppuccintheme)  [](https://fosstodon.org/@catppuccin)  [](https://reddit.com/r/catppuccin)
Join our community!
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 🧠 Design Philosophy
Content:
**Colorful is better than colorless** : the colorfulness of something contributes to the distinction amongst the parts
of that *something* , making it marginally easier to understand how things are structured.
**There should be balance** : not too dull, not too bright. Suitability under various light conditions is a must.
**Harmony is superior to dissonance** : vivacious colors must complement each other.
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 🎨 Palette
Content:
**Catppuccin** consists of 4 beautiful pastel color palettes, named **flavors** . All the details can be found below.
If you want to use them for your own project, refer to our [style guide](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/blob/main/docs/style-guide.md) for general use cases and guidelines. Additionally, you can find integrations with popular frameworks and tools in [catppuccin/palette](https://github.com/catppuccin/palette) .
Already have a project making use of our palette? Don't forget to add it to
our showcase section below!
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 👐 Contributing
Content:
Note
If you would like to submit a port or learn how to port Catppuccin to a program, please refer to
the [port-creation.md](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/blob/main/docs/port-creation.md)
See [CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 🪵 Ports and more!
Content:
Catppuccin is available for various apps and in different formats. Here is a list of them:
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 🌟 Showcase
Content:
Note
Catppuccin staff reserve the right to remove and reject showcase additions if we determine the addition to be in
violation of our [CODE OF CONDUCT](https://github.com/catppuccin/.github/blob/main/CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md) . Extremely
personal configurations and websites will **not** be added.
If you're making an application or tool using our palette, please let us know by adding it below!
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 🌟 Showcase
Content:
[flotes.app](https://flotes.app/) - A free note-taking app enhanced with flashcard features. [AnuPpuccin](https://github.com/AnubisNekhet/AnuPpuccin) - Highly customizable theme for [Obsidian](https://obsidian.md) . [faerber](https://farbenfroh.io/faerber) - Website for applying custom color schemes to any wallpaper. [Simple MP](https://github.com/lighttigerXIV/SimpleMP-Compose) - A simple music player based on Material You design. [Comfy](https://github.com/Comfy-Themes/Spicetify) - A theme for [Spicetify](https://github.com/spicetify/spicetify-cli) with a basic catppuccin color scheme! [Catppuccin Noctis](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=AlexDauenhauer.catppuccin-noctis) - An alternative to the official VSCode theme, with [Noctis](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=liviuschera.noctis) syntax highlighting. [Catppuccin Noctis Icons](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=AlexDauenhauer.catppuccin-noctis-icons) - A companion icons theme for Catppuccin Noctis color theme. Forked from [Symbols Icon Theme](https://github.com/miguelsolorio/vscode-symbols) . [Mind Elixir](https://github.com/ssshooter/mind-elixir-core) - A framework agnostic JavaScript mind map core. [Career Vault](https://careervault.io) - A remote job board that shows hundreds of new opportunities every day.
Section Title: catppuccin/catppuccin > Repository files navigation > 🌟 Showcase
Content:
[Loungy](https://loungy.app) - An open-source application launcher in the vein of Raycast and Alfred. [Reciper](https://github.com/judemont/reciper) - A simple, open-source recipe management app. [Catbbrew](https://catbbrew.com/) - Easily create your own Catppuccin flavors. [Tomatillo Timer](https://timer.flotes.app/?theme=mocha) - A modern pomodoro timer that syncs to your music
[Catppuccin Startpage](https://github.com/pivoshenko/catppuccin-startpage) - Aesthetic and clean startpage in Catppuccin style, hosted on GitHub Pages
[Gith](https://github.com/a3chron/gith) - A simple TUI git helper
[n-recipe](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.a3chron.nrecipe) - Recipe book for Android with Catppuccin & Material You themes
[Bookbank](https://github.com/alwaysnur/bookbank) - A self-hosted audiobook and podcast media server.

Section Title: Contribute > Readme
Content:
Dracula Theme is an open-source project driven by and for the community. Most apps that support the theme are contributions from our community.
As much as the team is responsible for the core theme and wants to support all available applications, we simply don't have the capacity to do it all ourselves.
That's why the community is essential for this project to keep evolving. Below are some guidelines, tips, and specifications for those who want to apply the theme to any application.
Section Title: Contribute > Readme > How do I submit a new theme?
Content:
1
Create a new repository based on
[this template.](https://github.com/dracula/template)
You can do it by cloning the repository or just clicking on "Use this template." 2
Build the theme using the
Color Palette below.
Edit the template info and upload the file with the theme configs to the repository. 3
Finally, [submit an issue](https://github.com/dracula/dracula-theme/issues/new)
with a link to your repository.
We will move the repo under the Dracula org and give you permissions to maintain it.

Section Title: nordtheme/nord > Repository files navigation
Content:
README
Code of conduct
Contributing
MIT license
Security
[](https://www.nordtheme.com)
[](https://github.com/nordtheme/nord/releases/latest) [](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs) [](https://github.com/nordtheme/nord/blob/develop/changelog.md)
[](https://www.npmjs.com/package/nord) [](https://www.npmjs.com/package/nord)
[](https://github.com/svengreb/styleguide-javascript/releases/latest) [](https://github.com/svengreb/styleguide-markdown/releases/latest) [](https://github.com/svengreb/styleguide-git/releases/latest)
An arctic, north-bluish color palette.
A total of sixteen, carefully selected, dimmed pastel colors for a eye-comfortable, but yet colorful ambiance.
Created for **clear, uncluttered and elegant designs** following a **minimal and flat** style pattern.
For syntax highlighting it aims to ensure an **undisturbed focus on important parts of the code** , a **good readability** and a **quick visual distinction** between the different syntax elements.
Nord consists of four named color palettes providing different syntactic meanings and color effects for dark & bright ambiance designs.
[](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/colors-and-palettes)
All colors are numbered from `nord0` to `nord15` where each palette contains a different amount of colors. The naming convention preserves the compatibility for terminal color schemes and allows an uncomplicated use as base for such.
Section Title: nordtheme/nord > Getting Started
Content:
Visit the [official website](https://www.nordtheme.com) to [learn all about Nord's colors and palettes](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/colors-and-palettes) and how to [install and integrate Nord in your own projects](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/usage) or use the [color swatches for your favorite applications](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/swatches) .
Section Title: nordtheme/nord > Getting Started > Quick Start
Content:
Install Nord using [npm](https://www.npmjs.com) or [yarn](https://yarnpkg.com) :
```
#  npm
npm install --save nord

 #  yarn
yarn add nord
```
Please see the complete [installation and usage guide](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/usage) for more details.
Section Title: nordtheme/nord > Port Projects
Content:
Unify the appearance and usage experience for your favorite applications — from code editors, shell terminals to modern UIs and libraries.
Nord supports a broad and constantly growing spectrum that allows to [customize your daily tool stack](https://www.nordtheme.com/ports) .
[](https://www.nordtheme.com/ports)
Section Title: nordtheme/nord > Color Swatches
Content:
Next to the many ways of [integrating Nord into your project](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/usage) , all [color palettes of Nord](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/colors-and-palettes) are also available in [various native color swatch formats](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/swatches) .
Easily import Nord into macOS, *Adobe* products like *Photoshop* & *Illustrator* , GIMP/Krita/Inkscape and many more.
[](https://www.nordtheme.com/docs/swatches)

Section Title: morhetz/gruvbox > Repository files navigation
Content:
README
[](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/02cc138f92841bc2be759f6046173315d01561d5dbd9961621873b2f8e058d25/68747470733a2f2f6d6f726865747a2e636f6d2f67727576626f782e737667)
gruvbox is heavily inspired by [badwolf](https://github.com/sjl/badwolf) , [jellybeans](https://github.com/nanotech/jellybeans.vim) and [solarized](http://ethanschoonover.com/solarized) .
Designed as a bright theme with pastel 'retro groove' colors and light/dark mode switching in the way of [solarized](http://ethanschoonover.com/solarized) . The main focus when developing gruvbox is to keep colors easily distinguishable, contrast enough and still pleasant for the eyes.
Section Title: morhetz/gruvbox > Attention
Content:
[Read this first](https://github.com/morhetz/gruvbox/wiki/Terminal-specific)
Typeface from gallery is [Fantasque Sans Mono](https://github.com/belluzj/fantasque-sans)
Typeface from screenshots below is [Fira Mono](https://mozilla.github.io/Fira/)

Section Title: morhetz/gruvbox > Features
Content:
Lots of style-customization options (contrast, color invertion, italics usage etc.)
Extended filetype highlighting: Html, Xml, Vim, Clojure, C, Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, PureScript, CoffeeScript, Ruby, Objective-C, Go, Lua, MoonScript, Java, Markdown, Haskell, Elixir
Supported plugins: [EasyMotion](https://github.com/Lokaltog/vim-easymotion) , [vim-sneak](https://github.com/justinmk/vim-sneak) , [Indent Guides](https://github.com/nathanaelkane/vim-indent-guides) , [indentLine](https://github.com/Yggdroot/indentLine) , [Rainbow Parentheses](https://github.com/kien/rainbow_parentheses.vim) , [Airline](https://github.com/bling/vim-airline) , [Lightline](https://github.com/itchyny/lightline.vim) , [GitGutter](https://github.com/airblade/vim-gitgutter) , [Signify](https://github.com/mhinz/vim-signify) , [ShowMarks](http://www.vim.org/scripts/script.php?script_id=152) , [Signature](https://github.com/kshenoy/vim-signature) , [Syntastic](https://github.com/scrooloose/syntastic) , [Ale](https://github.com/w0rp/ale) , [CtrlP](https://github.com/kien/ctrlp.vim) , [Startify](https://github.com/mhinz/vim-startify) , [NERDTree](https://github.com/scrooloose/nerdtree) , [Dirvish](https://github.com/justinmk/vim-dirvish)

Section Title: [Tokyo Night](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=enkia.tokyo-night) > Enabling JSDoc Highlights
Content:
Paste this into your [settings.json](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/getstarted/settings) to Enable JSDoc Highlight.
```
"editor.tokenColorCustomizations" : {
    "[Tokyo Night]" : { // or "[Tokyo Night Storm]"
        "textMateRules" : [
        {
            "scope" : [
                "comment keyword.codetag.notation" ,
                "comment.block.documentation keyword" ,
                "comment.block.documentation storage.type.class"
            ] ,
            "settings" : {
                "foreground" : ""
            }
        } ,
        {
            "scope" : [ "comment.block.documentation entity.name.type.instance" ] ,
            "settings" : {
                "foreground" : "" ,
                "fontStyle" : "italic"
            }
        } ,
        {
            "scope" : [
            "comment.block.documentation entity.name.type punctuation.definition.bracket"
            ] ,
            "settings" : {
                "foreground" : ""
            }
        } ,
        {
            "scope" : [ "comment.block.documentation variable" ] ,
            "settings" : {
                "foreground" : "" ,
                "fontStyle" : "italic"
            }
        }
        ]
    }
  }
```
 ...
Section Title: [Tokyo Night](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=enkia.tokyo-night) > ... > Higher Contrast Settings > Brightening Codelens text
Content:
I prefer my Codelens text fade into the background unless hovered over, but if you'd like a higher contrast, add this to your settings.json:
```
"workbench.colorCustomizations" : {
    "[Tokyo Night]" : { // or "[Tokyo Night Storm]"
        "editorCodeLens.foreground" : "" , // Preferred hex color
    }
 }
```
 ...

Section Title: Contribute > Readme
Content:
Dracula Theme is an open-source project driven by and for the community. Most apps that support the theme are contributions from our community.
As much as the team is responsible for the core theme and wants to support all available applications, we simply don't have the capacity to do it all ourselves.
That's why the community is essential for this project to keep evolving. Below are some guidelines, tips, and specifications for those who want to apply the theme to any application.
Section Title: Contribute > Readme > How do I submit a new theme?
Content:
1
Create a new repository based on
[this template.](https://github.com/dracula/template)
You can do it by cloning the repository or just clicking on "Use this template." 2
Build the theme using the
Color Palette below.
Edit the template info and upload the file with the theme configs to the repository. 3
Finally, [submit an issue](https://github.com/dracula/dracula-theme/issues/new)
with a link to your repository.
We will move the repo under the Dracula org and give you permissions to maintain it.

Section Title: General Usage
Content:
[!IMPORTANT]
Text colors are guidelines, certain cases require deviations
from the guidelines below. An example would be `text` on colored backgrounds.
Legibility always comes first, so please use your own judgement.

Nord
Ports Docs Blog Community
Ports Docs Blog Community
Section Title: Colors and Palettes
Content:
A total of sixteen, carefully selected, dimmed pastel colors for a eye-comfortable, but yet colorful ambiance.
This is a *living document* which means it is **work in progress** , not completed yet and **can change at any time** while the project is still in [development major version *zero*](https://semver.org/) `0.y.z` !
Nord consists of four named color palettes providing different syntactic meanings and color effects for dark & bright ambiance designs.
It has been created for **clear, uncluttered and elegant designs** following a **minimal and flat** style pattern.
For syntax highlighting it aims to ensure an **undisturbed focus on important parts of the code** , a **good readability** and a **quick visual distinction** between the different syntax elements.
All colors are numbered from `nord0` to `nord15` where each palette contains a different amount of colors. The naming convention preserves the compatibility for terminal color schemes and allows an uncomplicated use as base for such.
This document provides a set of guidelines detailing how to style and highlight code syntax and UI elements.
Please note that these guidelines are “soft“ recommendations to describe the original idea of Nord!
They may be used in a modified form like e.g. different use cases for a specific color or with different shading options applied.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes
Content:
This is a *living document* which means it is **work in progress** , not completed yet and **can change at any time** while the project is still in development!
Make sure to check out the guides about how to
install and integrate Nord in your own projects
or use the
color swatches for your favorite applications
.
Polar Night
\
\
\
\
**Polar Night** is made up of four darker colors that are commonly used for base elements like backgrounds or text color in *bright ambiance designs* .
 ...
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord1`
Content:
**A brighter shade color based on `nord0` .**
For *dark ambiance designs* it is used for elevated, more prominent or focused UI elements like
status bars and text editor gutters
panels, modals and floating popups like notifications or auto completion
user interaction/form components like buttons, text/select fields or checkboxes
It also works fine for more inconspicuous and passive elements like borders or as dropshadow between different components.
There's currently no official port project that makes use of it for syntax highlighting.
For *bright ambiance designs* , it is used for more subtle/inconspicuous UI text elements that do not need so much visual attention.
Other use cases are also state animations like a more brighter text color when a button is hovered, active or focused.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord2`
Content:
**An even more brighter shade color of `nord0` .**
For *dark ambiance designs* , it is used to colorize the currently active text editor line as well as selection- and text highlighting color.
For both *bright & dark ambiance designs* it can also be used as an brighter variant for the same target elements like `nord1` .
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord3`
Content:
**The brightest shade color based on `nord0` .**
For *dark ambiance designs* , it is used for UI elements like indent- and wrap guide marker.
In the context of code syntax highlighting it is used for comments and invisible/non-printable characters.
For *bright ambiance designs* , it is, next to `nord1` and `nord2` as darker variants, also used for the most subtle/inconspicuous UI text elements that do not need so much visual attention.
Snow Storm
\
\
\
**Snow Storm** is made up of three bright colors that are commonly used for text colors or base UI elements in *bright ambiance designs* .
The palette can be used for two different shading ambiance styles:
bright to dark — This is the **recommended style** that uses `nord6` as base color and highlights other UI elements with `nord5` and `nord4` .
dark to bright ( *semi-light* ) — This style uses `nord4` as base color and highlights other UI elements with `nord5` and `nord6` .
The documentation of the different colors from this palette are based on the recommended *bright to dark* ambiance style.
To apply the color purposes to the *dark to bright* style the definitions can be used in reversed order.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord4`
Content:
**The origin color or the *Snow Storm* palette.**
For *dark ambiance designs* , it is used for UI elements like the text editor caret.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used as text color for variables, constants, attributes and fields.
For *bright ambiance designs* , it is used for elevated, more prominent or focused UI elements like
status bars and text editor gutters
panels, modals and floating popups like notifications or auto completion
user interaction/form components like buttons, text/select fields or checkboxes
It also works fine for more inconspicuous and passive elements like borders or as dropshadow between different components.
In the context of syntax highlighting it's not used at all.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord5`
Content:
**A brighter shade color of `nord4` .**
For *dark ambiance designs* , it is used for more subtle/inconspicuous UI text elements that do not need so much visual attention.
Other use cases are also state animations like a more brighter text color when a button is hovered, active or focused.
For *bright ambiance designs* , it is used to colorize the currently active text editor line as well as selection- and text highlighting color.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord6`
Content:
**The brightest shade color based on `nord4` .**
For *dark ambiance designs* , it is used for elevated UI text elements that require more visual attention.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used as text color for plain text as well as reserved and structuring syntax characters like curly- and square brackets.
For *bright ambiance designs* , it is used as background and area coloring while it's not used for syntax highlighting at all because otherwise it would collide with the same background color.
Frost
\
\
\
\
**Frost** can be described as the heart palette of Nord, a group of four bluish colors that are commonly used for primary UI component and text highlighting and essential code syntax elements.
All colors of this palette are used the same for both *dark & bright ambiance designs* .
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord7`
Content:
**A calm and highly contrasted color reminiscent of frozen polar water.**
Used for UI elements that should, next to the primary accent color `nord8` , stand out and get more visual attention.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used for classes, types and primitives.
 ...
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord9`
Content:
**A more darkened and less saturated color reminiscent of arctic waters.**
Used for secondary UI elements that also require more visual attention than other elements.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used for language specific, syntactic and reserved keywords as well as
support characters
operators
tags
units
punctuations like (semi)colons, points and commas
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord10`
Content:
**A dark and intensive color reminiscent of the deep arctic ocean.**
Used for tertiary UI elements that require more visual attention than default elements.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used for pragmas, comment keywords and pre-processor statements.
Aurora
\
\
\
\
\
**Aurora** consists of five colorful components reminiscent of the [„Aurora borealis“](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurora) , sometimes referred to as *polar lights* or *northern lights* .
All colors of this palette are used the same for both *dark & bright ambiance designs* .
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord11`
Content:
Used for UI elements that are rendering error states like linter markers and the highlighting of Git `diff` deletions.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used to override the highlighting of syntax elements that are detected as errors.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord12`
Content:
Rarely used for UI elements, but it may indicate a more advanced or dangerous functionality.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used for special syntax elements like annotations and decorators.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord13`
Content:
Used for UI elements that are rendering warning states like linter markers and the highlighting of Git `diff` modifications.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used to override the highlighting of syntax elements that are detected as warnings as well as escape characters and within regular expressions.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord14`
Content:
Used for UI elements that are rendering success states and visualizations and the highlighting of Git `diff` additions.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used as main color for strings of any type like double/single quoted or interpolated.
Section Title: Colors and Palettes > `nord0` > `nord15`
Content:
Rarely used for UI elements, but it may indicate a more uncommon functionality.
In the context of syntax highlighting it is used as main color for numbers of any type like integers and floating point numbers.

Section Title: 1 Prelude > 1.1 Interpreting this specification
Content:
Language syntaxes and scopes will be referred to in the following manner throughout this document:
ScopeOfInterest
( ForegroundColor , BackgroundColor opt ) Italic opt Bold opt
Note Throughout the document, broad ranges of syntax tokens will be referred to using [TextMate Naming Conventions](http://manual.macromates.com/en/language_grammars)

Invalid
( Foreground , Red )

Section Title: 1 Prelude > 2 General > 2.2 Markup (Markdown, RST, etc.)
Content:
MarkupBold
( Orange ) Bold
MarkupHeading
( Purple ) Bold
MarkupItalic
( Yellow ) Italic
MarkupListBulletOrNumber
( Cyan )
MarkupInlineCode
( Green )
MarkupLinkUrl
( Cyan )
MarkupLinkText
( Pink )
MarkupBlockquote
( Yellow ) Italic
MarkupHorizontalRule
( Comment )
MarkupCodeBlockWithoutSyntax
( Orange )
MarkupRSTConstants
( Purple )
Note Braces and parens should be the same color as the foreground color of the currently scoped position in the document (e.g. Purple for headings, Foreground for regular text)
Section Title: 1 Prelude > 3 Classes
Content:
ClassName
( Cyan )
InstanceReservedWords
( Purple ) Italic
Note InstanceReservedWords refers to reserved words that the language uses to interact with the instance (e.g. `this` , `self` , `super` ).
InheritedClassName
( Cyan ) Italic
Section Title: 1 Prelude > 4 Comments
Content:
Comment
( Comment )
DocCommentKeywords
( Pink )
DocCommentTypes
( Cyan ) Italic
DocCommentParameters
( Orange ) Italic

Content:
```
/**
 *  @param { string } foo  - Some string parameter.
// ^^^^^^ -------------------------------------- Pink
//        ^      ^ ----------------------------- Cyan
//         ^^^^^^ ------------------------------ Cyan Italic
//                 ^^^ ------------------------- Orange Italic
 */
```

Content:

( Pink )
Note SeparatorsReferencesOrAccessors does not refer to simple object or class dot accessors.

Note GenericTemplatesAndMappedDeclarations would also color `P` and `T` on the LHS and `T` in the declaration of the following expression.

/// An arctic, north-bluish color palette.
/// Created for the clean- and minimal flat design pattern to achieve a optimal focus and readability for code syntax
/// highlighting and UI.
/// It consists of a total of sixteen, carefully selected, dimmed pastel colors for a eye-comfortable, but yet colorful
/// ambiance.
///
/// @author Sven Greb development@svengreb.de
////
/// Base component color of "Polar Night".
///
/// Used for texts, backgrounds, carets and structuring characters like curly- and square brackets.
///
/// @access public
/// @example scss - SCSS
///   /* For dark ambiance themes */
///   .background {
///     background-color: $nord0;
///   }
///   /* For light ambiance themes */
///   .text {
///     color: $nord0;
///   }
/// @group polarnight
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord0: #2e3440;
/// Lighter shade color of the base component color.
///
/// Used as a lighter background color for UI elements like status bars.
///
/// @access public
/// @group polarnight
/// @see $nord0
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord1: #3b4252;
/// Lighter shade color of the base component color.
///
/// Used as line highlighting in the editor.
/// In the UI scope it may be used as selection- and highlight color.
///
/// @access public
/// @example scss - SCSS
///   /* Code Syntax Highlighting scope */
///   .editor {
///     &.line {
///       background-color: $nord2;
///     }
///   }
///
///   /* UI scope */
///   button {
///     &:selected {
///       background-color: $nord2;
///     }
///   }
/// @group polarnight
/// @see $nord0
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord2: #434c5e;
/// Lighter shade color of the base component color.
///
/// Used for comments, invisibles, indent- and wrap guide marker.
/// In the UI scope used as pseudoclass color for disabled elements.
///
/// @access public
/// @example scss - SCSS
///   /* Code Syntax Highlighting scope */
///   .editor {
///     &.indent-guide,
///     &.wrap-guide {
///       &.marker {
///         color: $nord3;
///       }
///     }
///   }
///   .comment,
///   .invisible {

///

///     }
///   }
/// @group polarnight
/// @see $nord0
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord3: #4c566a;

///
/// Main color for text, variables, constants and attributes.
/// In the UI scope used as semi-light background depending on the theme shading design.
///
/// @access public
/// @example scss - SCSS
///   /* For light ambiance themes */

///     background-color: $nord4;

///   /* For dark ambiance themes */

/// @group snowstorm

$nord4: #d8dee9;

///
/// @access public
/// @group snowstorm
/// @see $nord4

$nord5: #e5e9f0;
/// Lighter shade color of the base component color.
///
/// Used for punctuations, carets and structuring characters like curly- and square brackets.

///

$nord6: #eceff4;
/// Bluish core color.

/// @access public
/// @group frost

$nord7: #8fbcbb;
/// Bluish core accent color.
///
/// Represents the accent color of the color palette.
/// Main color for primary UI elements and methods/functions.
///
/// Can be used for
///   - Markup quotes
///   - Markup link URLs
///
/// @access public
/// @group frost
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord8: #88c0d0;
/// Bluish core color.
///
/// Used for language-specific syntactic/reserved support characters and keywords, operators, tags, units and
/// punctuations like (semi)colons,commas and braces.
///
/// @access public
/// @group frost
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord9: #81a1c1;
/// Bluish core color.
///
/// Used for markup doctypes, import/include/require statements, pre-processor statements and at-rules (`@`).
///
/// @access public
/// @group frost
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord10: #5e81ac;
/// Colorful component color.

/// Used for errors, git/diff deletion and linter marker.
///
/// @access public
/// @group aurora
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord11: #bf616a;
/// Colorful component color.
///
/// Used for annotations.
///
/// @access public
/// @group aurora
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord12: #d08770;
/// Colorful component color.
///
/// Used for escape characters, regular expressions and markup entities.
/// In the UI scope used for warnings and git/diff renamings.
///
/// @access public
/// @group aurora
/// @since 0.1.0
$nord13: #ebcb8b;
/// Colorful component color.
///
/// Main color for strings and attribute values.

$nord14: #a3be8c;
/// Colorful component color.

Your theme system is well-structured — YAML files with `ui` (editor, gutter, status_bar, sidebar, tab_bar, overlay, csv) and `syntax` sections. All colors are hex strings resolved to `Color` RGBA at load time.

#### Gap Analysis
You have **3 dark, 1 light**, and all are relatively "corporate/neutral" palettes. You're missing:
- **Community cult favorites** (Dracula, Catppuccin, Gruvbox)
- **Muted/aesthetic** themes (Nord)
- **Vibrant/modern** themes (Tokyo Night)

#### 5 Recommended Themes

**1. 🧛 Dracula** (23k+ GitHub stars) — The most iconic community theme ever. Bold purples, pinks, cyans on a deep purple-gray background. Instantly recognizable.
- BG: `#282A36`, FG: `#F8F8F2`, Comment: `#6272A4`, Keywords: `#FF79C6` (pink), Strings: `#F1FA8C` (yellow), Functions: `#50FA7B` (green), Types: `#8BE9FD` (cyan), Constants: `#BD93F9` (purple)

**2. 🐱 Catppuccin Mocha** (18k+ stars) — The hottest modern theme. Soothing pastels that are warm yet readable. Beloved by the Neovim/ricing community.
- BG: `#1E1E2E`, FG: `#CDD6F4`, Keywords: `#CBA6F7` (mauve), Strings: `#A6E3A1` (green), Functions: `#89B4FA` (blue), Types: `#F9E2AF` (yellow), Comments: `#6C7086`, Numbers: `#FAB387` (peach)

**3. 🏔️ Nord** (6.8k stars) — Arctic, minimal, dimmed pastels. Beloved for its calm, professional feel. Popular across every editor.
- BG: `#2E3440`, FG: `#D8DEE9`, Keywords: `#81A1C1`, Strings: `#A3BE8C`, Functions: `#88C0D0`, Types: `#8FBCBB`, Comments: `#4C566A`, Numbers: `#B48EAD`, Errors: `#BF616A`

**4. 🌃 Tokyo Night** (2.3k stars, extremely popular on Neovim/VS Code) — Inspired by Tokyo's neon lights. Deep indigo background with vibrant yet harmonious highlights.
- BG: `#1A1B26`, FG: `#A9B1D6`, Keywords: `#F7768E` (red), Strings: `#9ECE6A` (green), Functions: `#7AA2F7` (blue), Types: `#E0AF68` (yellow), Comments: `#565F89`, Numbers: `#FF9E64` (orange)

**5. 🎸 Gruvbox Dark** (15.2k stars) — Retro groove, warm earthy tones. A Vim classic that's spread everywhere. Has a unique "analog" warmth no other theme matches.
- BG: `#282828`, FG: `#EBDBB2`, Keywords: `#FB4934` (red), Strings: `#B8BB26` (green), Functions: `#FABD2F` (yellow), Types: `#83A598` (aqua), Comments: `#928374`, Numbers: `#D3869B` (purple), Constants: `#D79921`

#### Why These 5?

1. **Popularity** — All are top-10 most-used themes across VS Code, Neovim, JetBrains, Sublime, and terminal emulators
2. **Diversity** — Covers warm retro (Gruvbox), cool minimal (Nord), vibrant modern (Tokyo Night), pastel cozy (Catppuccin), and bold iconic (Dracula)
3. **Community expectation** — Users switching from other editors will look for these first
4. **Complementary** — They fill gaps in your current palette: you have no warm themes, no pastel themes, and no vibrant community themes

on GitHub\nContent:\nNote that through the magic of [git-subtree](https://github.com/apenwarr/git-subtree) these repositories are all kept in sync, so you can pull any of them and get the most up-to-date version.\nSection Title: Solarized > Features\nContent:\n**Selective contrast**On a sunny summer day I love to read a book outside. Not right in the sun;\nthat’s too bright. I’ll hunt for a shady spot under a tree. The shaded\npaper contrasts with the crisp text nicely. If you were to actually measure\nthe contrast between the two, you’d find it is much lower than black text\non a white background (or white on black) on your display device of choice. Black text on white from a computer display is akin to reading a book in\ndirect sunlight and tires the eye.Solarized reduces *brightness contrast* but, unlike many low contrast\ncolorschemes, retains *contrasting hues* (based on colorwheel relations)\nfor syntax highlighting readability. **Both sides of the force**I often switch between dark and light modes when editing text and code. Solarized retains the same selective contrast relationships and overall\nfeel when switching between the light and dark background modes.\nSection Title: Solarized > Features\nContent:\nA *lot* of\nthought, planning and testing has gone into making both modes feel like\npart of a unified colorscheme. **16 ⁄ 5 palette modes**Solarized works as a sixteen color palette for compatibility with common\nterminal based applications / emulators. In addition, it has been carefully\ndesigned to scale down to a variety of five color palettes (four base\nmonotones plus one accent color) for use in design work such as web design. In every case it retains a strong personality but doesn’t overwhelm. **Precision, symmetry**The monotones have symmetric CIELAB lightness differences, so switching\nfrom dark to light mode retains the same perceived contrast in brightness\nbetween each value. Each mode is equally readable. The accent colors are\nbased off specific colorwheel relations and subsequently translated to\nCIELAB to ensure perceptual uniformity in terms of lightness.\nSection Title: Solarized > Features\nContent:\nThe hues\nthemselves, as with the monotone *a*b values, have been adjusted within\na small range to achieve the most pleasing combination of colors.See also the Usage & Development section below for\ndetails on the specific values to be used in different contexts.This makes colorscheme inversion trivial. Here, for instance, is a sass\n(scss) snippet that inverts solarized based on the class of the html tag\n(e.g. `<html class=\"dark red\">` to give a dark background with red accent):See also [the full css stylesheet for this site](https://github.com/altercation/ethanschoonover.com/blob/master/resources/css/style.css) .\n ... \nSection Title: Solarized > Font Samples\nContent:\nSolarized has been designed to handle fonts of various weights and retain\nreadability, from the classic Terminus to the beefy Menlo.\nClockwise from upper left: Menlo, Letter Gothic, Terminus, Andale Mono.\nPreview all The website's theme system is centered around a central data configuration and a dedicated preview page that simulates an IDE environment.

### Theme Data & Config
The themes (including "Fleet Dark", "Default Dark", "GitHub Dark", and "GitHub Light") are defined in a central data file which includes color hex codes for editor, chrome, and syntax highlighting.
- [website/src/data/themes.ts#L44-L177](file://~/code/token-editor/website/src/data/themes.ts#L44-L177)

### Theme Preview & UI
The `/themes` page is the primary location where themes are listed and displayed. It features:
- **Sidebar**: Lists available themes as files in a mock explorer. [website/src/pages/themes.astro#L47-L60](file://~/code/token-editor/website/src/pages/themes.astro#L47-L60)
- **Editor Preview**: A mock code editor that applies theme colors via CSS variables. [website/src/pages/themes.astro#L83-L94](file://~/code/token-editor/website/src/pages/themes.astro#L83-L94)
- **Detail Pane**: Shows theme metadata and color swatches. [website/src/pages/themes.astro#L99-L178](file://~/code/token-editor/website/src/pages/themes.astro#L99-L178)
- **Theme Switching Logic**: JavaScript that maps theme data to CSS custom properties (e.g., `--theme-bg`, `--theme-keyword`) to update the UI dynamically. [website/src/pages/themes.astro#L212-L260](file://~/code/token-editor/website/src/pages/themes.astro#L212-L260)

### Styling
Themes are applied using scoped CSS variables within the IDE frame component on the themes page.
- [website/src/pages/themes.astro#L475-L568](file://~/code/token-editor/website/src/pages/themes.astro#L475-L568)
```

Section Title: projekt0n/github-nvim-theme > Supported Colorschemes & Comparisons
Content:
| github-nvim-theme | github-vscode-theme | Github Web UI | Primer |
| github_dark | Github Dark | - | - |
| github_light | Github Light | - | - |
| github_dark_dimmed | Github Dark Dimmed | Dark dimmed | dark_dimmed |
| github_dark_default | Github Dark Default | Dark default | dark |
| github_light_default | Github Light Default | Light default | light |
| github_dark_high_contrast | Github Dark High Contrast | Dark high contrast | dark_high_contrast |
| github_light_high_contrast | Github Light High Contrast | Light high contrast | light_high_contrast |
| github_dark_colorblind | Github Dark Colorblind (Beta) | Dark Protanopia & Deuteranopia | dark_colorblind |
| github_light_colorblind | Github Light Colorblind (Beta) | Light Protanopia & Deuteranopia | light_colorblind |
| github_dark_tritanopia | - | Dark Tritanopia | dark_tritanopia |
| github_light_tritanopia | - | Light Tritanopia | light_tritanopia |
Section Title: projekt0n/github-nvim-theme > Features
Content:
Supports the latest Neovim>=0.5 features like Treesitter and LSP
Resembles GitHub's VSCode themes and follows [primer design guidelines](https://primer.style/primitives/storybook)
Has Github's colorblind themes
Support for multiple plugins and status lines
And many others should 'just work'!
Minimal inactive Vim's Default statusline
Vim terminal colors
Darker background for sidebar-like windows
Highly configurable with template overriding
Most elegant lualine theme
Compile user's configuration for fast startup times
Export Color library utility
Interactive live config re-loading
Section Title: projekt0n/github-nvim-theme > Requirements
Content:
Neovim >= 0.8.0 (for older version use [0.0.x](https://github.com/projekt0n/github-nvim-theme/tree/0.0.x) branch)
True color support
Undercurl terminal support (optional)
Note
On macOS, the default terminal (i.e. `Terminal.app` ) does not support ***true color*** 24-bit RGB colors. Use either `Iterm2` or [another] terminal which supports ***true color*** .
 ...
Section Title: projekt0n/github-nvim-theme > Usage
Content:
Simply set the colorscheme with the builtin command `:colorscheme`
```
colorscheme github_dark
```
```
vim . cmd ( ' colorscheme github_dark ' )
```
 ...
